Stop only at a real safety, security, authorization, destructive-action, or impossible-contract boundary.\\n---\\nname: systematic-debugging\\ndescription: Use to find the cause of a bug, test failure, slowdown, or unexpected result\\n---\\n\\n# Systematic Debugging\\n\\nFind the cause before changing the code. The method is evidence first: reproduce, trace, form one hypothesis, make one grounded fix, and verify the original symptom.\\n\\nScale the investigation to the problem. If an error, diff, or reproduction already proves a simple local cause, explain it and fix it directly. A typo, wrong selector, misplaced style, or clearly incorrect value does not need artificial ceremony.\\n\\n## Phase 1: Establish the Cause\\n\\n### Read the evidence\\n\\n- Read the full error, warning, stack trace, and relevant logs.\\n- Record concrete paths, lines, error codes, inputs, and observed state.\\n- Separate what was observed from what is inferred.\\n\\n### Reproduce the symptom\\n\\n- Identify the smallest faithful sequence that triggers it.\\n- Check whether it is consistent or intermittent.\\n- If it is intermittent, record the conditions instead of hiding it with a longer sleep.\\n- Use `condition-based-waiting.md` when a fix would otherwise rely on a sleep or a raised timeout.\\n\\n### Check what changed\\n\\n- Inspect the relevant diff, recent commits, dependencies, configuration, and environment.\\n- Find a nearby working path and compare it with the broken one.\\n\\n### Trace the data or event\\n\\nFollow the bad value, state transition, or event backward until its source is known. For multi-component systems, inspect the smallest useful boundary first: what entered, what left, and which configuration or owner was active.\\n\\nAdd temporary diagnostics only when existing evidence cannot locate the failure. Do not instrument every layer by default.\\n\\nUse `root-cause-tracing.md` when the failure is deep in a call chain.\\n\\n## Phase 2: Compare Patterns\\n\\n1. Find similar working code in the same repository.\\n2. Compare inputs, ownership, lifecycle, configuration, ordering, and error handling.\\n3. List differences that could explain the symptom.\\n4. Read an external reference completely only when the implementation depends on that reference; do not load long material merely because it exists.\\n\\nThe result should be a concrete statement such as: “The stale response overwrites the new selection because this callback has no ownership check,” not “This is probably a race.”\\n\\n## Phase 3: Test One Hypothesis\\n\\nState one hypothesis and why the evidence supports it. Make the smallest change or observation that can confirm or reject it. Change one variable at a time.\\n\\nIf the hypothesis fails, remove or isolate the speculative change, record what was learned, and form a new hypothesis. Do not stack several guesses and then infer which one helped.\\n\\nFor timing and performance complaints, measure the real operation in the real environment. Do not invent an acceptable duration, token count, or failure threshold. A guessed CLI runtime is not a product requirement.\\n\\n## Phase 4: Fix and Verify\\n\\nFix the source of the problem rather than masking the symptom. Keep the change focused, then rerun the original reproduction and relevant surrounding checks.\\n\\nChoose evidence by boundary:\\n\\n| Problem | Verification |\\n|---|---|\\n| Database, data operation, API, persistence, migration, or contract | Minimal failing automated test, then TDD |\\n| Cross-process or async state | Boundary/event evidence plus a focused test when useful |\\n| Ordinary frontend or component behavior | Reproduce the interaction and run relevant existing checks; add tests when requested or materially useful |\\n| Pure styling, shape, size, position, or layout | Render and inspect affected viewports; no new automated test |\\n| Prose, prompt, or docs | Inspect the final text and diff |\\n| CLI duration or resource use | Measure a real run only when the user requested that claim |\\n\\nUse `test-driven-development` only for the infrastructure and data-contract cases it covers. A bug report does not automatically create a TDD requirement.\\n\\n## When Attempts Fail\\n\\nA failed fix is new evidence, not an automatic approval gate.\\n\\n- Return to the evidence and update the hypothesis.\\n- Check whether repeated failures point to shared state, coupling, or a wrong architectural assumption.\\n- Continue when a grounded, reversible next step exists.\\n- Ask the user only when the next step materially changes product behavior or architecture, needs new authority, or is destructive, security-sensitive, or externally visible.\\n\\nDo not stop merely because an arbitrary number of attempts elapsed. Do not keep guessing when no evidence supports another attempt.\\n\\n## Useful Red Flags\\n\\n- proposing fixes before reproducing or locating the failure,\\n- calling something a race without timing or event evidence,\\n- increasing a timeout without showing what condition is late,\\n- changing several causes at once,\\n- changing a test to accept behavior not established as correct,\\n- demanding an automated test for a cosmetic or prose correction,\\n- saying “environmental” without showing what was measured,\\n- trusting a previous agent's diagnosis without checking its evidence.\\n\\nWhen the user says “stop guessing,” “is that actually happening?”, or “why doesn't it work?”, return to observed evidence. Explain the proven cause in plain language and, when the fix is clear and in scope, implement it without another approval round.\\n\\nBefore claiming success, use `verification-before-completion` and show evidence tied to the original symptom.\\n---\\nname: client-server-state-invariants\\ndescription: Use for React server state, optimistic UI, caches, polling, streams, background jobs, persistence, or synchronization\\n---\\n\\n# Client Server State Invariants\\n\\nReact bugs often expose deeper ownership mistakes. When several client, server, cache, stream, or persisted authorities can race or overwrite one another, make the relevant invariants explicit. A simple fetch-and-display path does not need the whole checklist.\\n\\n## Core Rule\\n\\nNo UI surface may treat data as authoritative unless the payload proves ownership, freshness, completeness, ordering, and terminal state.\\n\\nIf those words are missing from the plan, the plan is probably baking in a race condition.\\n\\n## Planning Checklist\\n\\nFor the cross-boundary state that can affect correctness, answer the applicable questions:\\n\\n1. **Owner:** What owns this state: server, client manager, URL, cache, stream, local storage, or worker/runtime?\\n2. **Token:** What owner token protects async results: `requestId`, `runId`, `projectPath`, `operationId`, `workerId + seq`, or equivalent?\\n3. **Provenance:** Is data server-authoritative, optimistic, cached preview, partial update, or historical fallback?\\n4. **Completeness:** Does the payload declare what scope it is complete for? What may it erase?\\n5. **Ordering:** What stable order is used: monotonic seq, `(createdAt, id)`, version, or cursor?\\n6. **State machine:** What are the legal states, terminal states, and transitions?\\n7. **Events:** Which server decisions are named events rather than client-side derivations?\\n8. **Loading shape:** Which states are bootstrap, first load, incremental refresh, reconnect, resync, recovery, and terminal error?\\n9. **Fallbacks:** Which legacy/fallback rows can render, and only after what authority is loaded?\\n10. **Tests:** Which race, stale-response, event-sequence, reconnect, and merge tests prove the invariants?\\n\\n## Invariants To Require\\n\\n- Cached and optimistic data may pre-render, but cannot satisfy full-load gates.\\n- Partial snapshots may add or refine state, but may not erase optimistic objects, delivered user input, read markers, or stream entries unless they declare complete scope.\\n- Every async response checks that its owner token still matches before mutating visible state.\\n- Mutation success handlers may navigate or clear UI only if they still own the current context, unless the action explicitly creates a new selected context.\\n- Timers and polling callbacks re-check ownership immediately before acting.\\n- Server decisions are emitted as named events; the client does not infer decisions by diffing snapshots.\\n- SSE/WebSocket streams have event ids, replay/resume, and an explicit resync path.\\n- Loading booleans are split by lifecycle stage. A leaf stream refresh must not unmount the whole page.\\n- Append-only content has one writer, one reader, stable ids, and monotonic cursors.\\n- Fallback DB rows or historical rows are gated behind stream loading and deduped by stable id plus safe equivalence.\\n- Status classifiers distinguish active, runnable, blocked, awaiting user, terminal, stale, and recoverable states.\\n- \\\"Latest\\\" queries have deterministic tie-breakers. Timestamp-only ordering is not enough.\\n- Hot paths use bounded reads and do not synchronously load large bodies, bridge probes, schema setup, or heavy app graphs.\\n- Process-local caches have explicit TTL/size bounds and invalidation rules.\\n\\n## Test Matrix\\n\\nPick the rows that match the work. Do not rely on one happy-path UI test.\\n\\n| Risk | Test shape |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| Stale async response | Start request A, switch owner, resolve A, assert it cannot mutate B |\\n| Optimistic object erased | Create local optimistic state, apply stale/partial snapshot, assert object remains |\\n| Cache masquerades as loaded | Hydrate cache preview, assert full-load gate waits for server authority |\\n| Mutation steals navigation | Start mutation, switch selection, resolve mutation, assert focus stays put |\\n| Timer/poll stale-fire | Use fake timers or deferred promises; callback must re-check owner before acting |\\n| Snapshot merge erases stream | Apply partial snapshot missing known entries, assert entries/read markers remain |\\n| Event stream gap | Bootstrap snapshot, emit events during disconnect, reconnect with last id, assert replay or resync |\\n| Server decision invisible | Exercise the branch and assert a named event such as `action.blocked`, `request.rejected`, `gave_up`, or `error.surfaced` |\\n| State classifier wrong | Table-test every status into active/runnable/terminal/recoverable buckets |\\n| Fallback duplicates stream | Load fallback rows then stream entries, assert no duplicate or false chronology |\\n| Hot path bloats | Assert snapshots carry cursors not bodies; route returns before DB/heavy imports when possible |\\n| Timestamp tie | Insert equal timestamps, assert deterministic order by id/seq |\\n| Context switch journey | If the user approves a high-risk browser journey, start work in A, switch to B before completion, return to both, and inspect visible ownership and status |\\n| Background and return | If the user approves a high-risk browser journey, background the app while polling/streaming continues, return, and inspect freshness and continuity |\\n\\nUse headless HTTP/SSE tests for control-plane decisions and focused React/unit tests for Manager merging and ownership guards. For ordinary UI behavior, reproduce the affected interaction directly. Use `agentic-user-journey-testing` for a high-risk composed browser sequence only after explicit user approval. Do not ask a DOM test alone to prove a server decision.\\n\\n## Avoid These Shapes\\n\\n- one `isLoading` boolean for bootstrap, refresh, reconnect, and recovery;\\n- cache or optimistic data treated as authoritative without provenance;\\n- async callbacks that mutate state without checking owner tokens;\\n- client-side derivation of server decisions;\\n- snapshot diffs used as the only way to know what happened;\\n- event streams without ids or resume;\\n- timestamp-only \\\"latest\\\" queries;\\n- parallel transcript or persistence surfaces that the frontend must reconcile;\\n- unbounded process caches introduced to hide slow storage;\\n- tests that prove only the DOM result when the bug is a server decision.\\n\\n## Review Prompt\\n\\nAsk this before implementation starts:\\n\\n> What can arrive late, out of order, partially, twice, or after the user changes context, and what test proves it cannot corrupt visible state?\\n\"}}"

title\\\" }),\\n      sessionInfoEntry({ updatedAt: \\\"2026-08-05T12:00:00.000Z\\\" }),\\n    ])).toBe(\\\"Real title\\\");\\n  });\\n\\n  it(\\\"ignores blank titles\\\", () => {\\n expect(extractAgentSessionTitle([\\n      { id: randomUUID(), type: \\\"message\\\", text: \\\"hello\\\", raw: {} },\\n    ])).toBeNull();\\n    expect(extractAgentSessionTitle([])).toBeNull();\\n    expect(extractAgentSessionTitle(undefined)).toBeNull();\\n  });\\n});\\n\\ndescribe(\\\"applyAgentSessionTitle\\\", () => {\\n  beforeEach(async () => {\\n    await db.delete(runs);\\n    await db.delete(plans);\\n    __resetNamedEventsForTests();\\n  });\\n\\n  async function insertRun(title: string) {\\n    const planId = randomUUID();\\n    const runId = randomUUID();\\n    const now = new Date();\\n    await db.insert(plans).values({\\n      id: planId,\\n      path: `vibes/ad-hoc/${planId}.md`,\\n      status: \\\"running\\\",\\n      createdAt: now,\\n      updatedAt: now,\\n    });\\n    await db.insert(runs).values({\\n      id: runId,\\n      planId,\\n      mode: \\\"direct\\\",\\n      status: \\\"running\\\",\\n      projectPath: \\\"/workspace/app\\\",\\n }).from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get();\\n    return row?.title ?? null;\\n  }\\n\\n  it(\\\"replaces the first-message fallback title with the agent's title\\\", async () => {\\n    const runId = await insertRun(\\\"Sometimes I Send A Message And\\\");\\n\\n    await applyAgentSessionTitle({ runId, title: \\\"Duplicate sent-message race condition\\\" });\\n\\n    expect(await readTitle(runId)).toBe(\\\"Duplicate sent-message race condition\\\");\\n  });\\n\\n  it(\\\"emits a named event recording the adoption\\\", async () => {\\nimport { describe, expect, it, vi } from \\\"@/interface/home/LiveEventConnectionManager\\\";\\nimport type { EventStreamState } from \\\"@/interface/home/types\\\";\\n\\nfunction createState(id: string): EventStreamState {\\n  return {\\n Date(0).toISOString(),\\n      projectPath: null,\\n      title: id,\\n    }],\\n    accounts: [],\\n    agents: [],\\n    workers: [],\\n    planItems: [],\\n    clarifications: [],\\n    executionEvents: [],\\n    supervisorInterventions: [],\\n    frontendErrors: [],\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nfunction createWorkerEntriesNotifier() {\\n  return {\\n    onKnownSeqs: vi.fn(),\\n    onStreamResync: vi.fn(),\\n    onWakeUp: vi.fn(),\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nfunction createPlanNotifier() {\\n  return {\\n    onKnownSeqs: vi.fn(),\\n    onStreamResync: vi.fn(),\\n    onWakeUp: vi.fn(),\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nclass MockEventSource {\\n  static instances: MockEventSource[] = [];\\n\\n  readonly listeners = new Map<string, Array<(event: MessageEvent) => void>>();\\n  onerror: (() => void) | null = null;\\n  onopen: (() => void) | null = null;\\n  closed = false;\\n\\n  constructor(readonly url: string) {\\n    MockEventSource.instances.push(this);\\n  }\\n\\n  addEventListener(event: string, listener: (event: MessageEvent) => void) {\\n    this.listeners.set(event, [...(this.listeners.get(event) ?? []), listener]);\\n  }\\n\\n  emit(event: string, data: unknown, lastEventId = \\\"\\\") {\\n    for (const listener of this.listeners.get(event) ?? []) {\\n      listener({ data: JSON.stringify(data), lastEventId } as MessageEvent);\\n    }\\n  }\\n\\n  close() {\\n    this.closed = true;\\n  }\\n}\\n\\nasync function flushAsyncWork() {\\n  await vi.runAllTicks();\\n  for (let index = 0; index < 5; index += 1) {\\n    await Promise.resolve();\\n  }\\n  await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(0);\\n}\\n\\ndescribe(\\\"LiveEventConnectionManager\\\", () => {\\n  it(\\\"persists cursors behind an injected runner scope key\\\", () => {\\n    const values = new Map<string, string>();\\n    const storage = {\\n      getItem: (key: string) => values.get(key) ?? null,\\n      setItem: (key: string, value: string) => {\\n        values.set(key, value);\\n      },\\n      removeItem: (key: string) => {\\n        values.delete(key);\\n      },\\n    };\\n    const first = new LiveEventCursorManager(null, {\\n      scopeKey: \\\"runner-a\\\",\\n      storage,\\n    });\\n\\n    expect(first.advance(\\\"epoch-a:7\\\")).toBe(true);\\n    expect(values.get(\\\"omniharness:event-cursor:runner-a\\\")).toBe(\\\"epoch-a:7\\\");\\n\\n    const restored = new LiveEventCursorManager(null, {\\n      scopeKey: \\\"runner-a\\\",\\n      storage,\\n    });\\n    expect(restored.getCurrent()).toBe(\\\"epoch-a:7\\\");\\n\\n    restored.clear();\\n    expect(values.has(\\\"omniharness:event-cursor:runner-a\\\")).toBe(false);\\n  });\\n\\nimport { defineConfig } from \\\"vitest/config\\\";\\nimport path from \\\"path\\\";\\nimport tsconfigPaths from \\\"vite-tsconfig-paths\\\";\\n\\nexport default defineConfig({\\n  test: {\\n    environment: \\\"node\\\",\\n    setupFiles: [\\\"./tests/setup.ts\\\"],\\n    include: [\\\"tests/**/*.test.{ts,tsx}\\\"],\\n    exclude: [\\\"tests/e2e/**\\\"],\\n    // Lifecycle scenarios spawn subprocesses and assert against SSE\\n    // tails over real ports; the default 5s test timeout is too tight\\n    // under parallel CPU load. Bump to 20s as a floor — well-behaved\\n    // tests still finish in milliseconds.\\n    testTimeout: 20_000,\\n  },\\n  plugins: [tsconfigPaths()],\\n  resolve: {\\n    alias: {\\n      \\\"@\\\": path.resolve(__dirname, \\\"src\\\"),\\n    },\\n  },\\n});\\n---\\nname: sloptrim\\ndescription: >-\\n  Use when the user wants to humanize text, trim slop, de-AI or de-slop writing,\\n  remove AI tells, fix robotic or ChatGPT-sounding prose, or make writing sound\\n  human and natural. Also run before delivering a CV, cover letter, email,\\n  report, or essay to be sent. Removes 71 documented AI-writing patterns with a\\n  local detector, preserves numbers, names and citations, and rebuilds toward a\\n  human voice rather than a flat husk. Mode-aware, so it never fabricates voice\\n  on factual content.\\nversion: 0.9.0\\nlicense: Apache-2.0\\nallowed-tools: Read, Write, Edit, Grep, Glob, Bash\\nargument-hint: The text to trim, or a file path\\n---\\n\\n# Sloptrim\\n\\nTrims AI slop from text. Works a catalogue of 71 patterns: 62 machine-checked by `scripts/detect.py`, 9 requiring semantic judgment during the rewrite. Of the 62, 50 can move the score; the rest are reported as writing advice and count for nothing. Mode-aware so it does not invent voice on factual content. Preserves facts. Rebuilds the cleaned text toward a selectable human-voice **style profile** so it does not read as a sterile de-AI'd husk.\\n\\n## Silence\\n\\nThis skill never talks about itself. It hands back the cleaned text and nothing else.\\n\\n**Never emit:** any announcement that sloptrim ran, style/mode lines, scores or reports, drafts, self-audit notes, facts-preserved lines, change lists, preambles, closing offers.\\n\\n**Emit only:**\\n- Text given inline: the final text. Nothing before it, nothing after it.\\n- A file path given: rewrite the file in place, then one line - the path. Nothing else.\\n- Fired as a pre-delivery pass on something you were already writing (CV, cover letter, email, report, essay): just deliver the clean text. Do not mention that a pass happened.\\n\\nEvery internal step still runs - detector, tiering, self-audit, character scrub. Silence means nothing is *printed*, not that anything is skipped.\\n\\nBreak silence only for: (a) a fact you cannot preserve, or an unusual word you cannot verify against the domain - one line, then the text; (b) the user explicitly asking for the score, the report, a style choice, or a diff - then answer in full; (c) a **high-stakes first-person deliverable** - a cover letter, personal statement, or bio the user is about to send - where after the text you may add exactly one line offering a voice switch (for example: \\\"Voice here is plain-professional; say the word for warmer or more formal.\\\"). One line, only for these high-stakes cases, never for routine prose.\\n\\n## Style, chosen silently\\n\\nNever ask. Read the content and pick:\\n\\n- encyclopedic / factual / technical → **2000s textbook**\\n- first-person / opinion → **conversational essay**\\n- docs, README, business prose → **plain / clear**\\n- news → **journalistic**\\n\\nIf the user names a style or pastes a writing sample, that wins (see *Matching the user's own voice*).\\n\\n## Task\\n\\n1. **Pick the style silently** (above). Never ask.\\n2. **Classify the content** (see *Content type and voice*) - conservative or drastic mode. The style profile sets the *target* rhythm and register; the mode sets how far you may push voice. They compose: e.g. 2000s textbook always stays conservative; conversational essay implies drastic mode.\\n3. **Run `scripts/detect.py`** and tier the pass to `_metrics.ai_tell_score`: `clean` (≤20) - character scrub and flagged spans only, do NOT rewrite (a human-first draft keeps its voice); `light tells` (21-40) - targeted edits plus rhythm repair; `mixed` and above - full rewrite toward the style profile.\\n4. **Identify remaining patterns** that require semantic judgment. Pattern names and category map are below. Read `references/patterns.md` when you need the precise Before/After examples for any pattern.\\n5. **Note critical content to preserve:** numbers, proper nouns, hyphenated technical terms, citations, units, dates. Then note the things an entity list does not hold, from *Critical content preservation* below: which claims are attributed and which are the writer's own, which are hedged, which figures belong to which nouns, every placeholder, and the input word count you may not exceed.\\n6. **Produce a draft rewrite** toward the chosen style profile.\\n7. **Anti-sterility self-audit loop** - internal, never printed: re-run `python \\\"$DETECT\\\"` on the draft; ask what still reads as AI, and whether it over-flattened (`length_cv` < 0.35, uniform paragraphs, `readability_uniform` true, contractions gone in drastic mode - a flat husk reads as machine-made just as fast as slop). Fix only the offending spans; cap at two loops; accept at `clean`/`light tells` and not over-flattened. Check the other direction in the same pass: count the words. Past 1.25x the input you have written new material, and no score justifies keeping it.\\n8. **Verify:** every fact preserved, no fabricated dates / quotes / sources, and every unusual word is correct for the domain (a real but wrong word is worse than a typo). Then re-read for the six failures in *Critical content preservation*: no attribution added or removed, no last hedge cut, no criticism reading as praise, every figure still on its own noun, every placeholder untouched, and the word count inside 1.25x.\\n9. **Scrub the output:** re-run `python \\\"$DETECT\\\"` on the final text and confirm `invisible_chars`, `nonstandard_spaces`, and `homoglyphs` are `0` (#62/#66/#67/#68 silent). Drafts from other models can carry zero-width or TAG-block characters that survive copy-paste; `python \\\"$DETECT\\\" --clean` strips them, normalizes spaces, folds homoglyphs, and trims stray whitespace without touching visible content.\\n10. **Output the final text, and only the final text** (see *Silence*).\\n\\n## Style profiles\\n\\nRemoving AI tells is only half the job. The other half is rebuilding the text toward a voice a person would actually write in. Pick one from the content, silently (see *Style, chosen silently*). Each profile is a *positive target* - it does not change which AI tells are removed or the preservation rules; it sets the rhythm, register, punctuation, and paragraph shape the cleaned prose is rebuilt into.\\n\\n**1. 2000s textbook** *(default)* - pre-LLM human academic prose (a well-edited textbook, roughly 2000-2008). Clear declarative sentences, one idea each; real length variation (a short statement, a longer development, a worked example - never metronomic); concrete examples introduced naturally; occasional first-person-plural for exposition, never first-person-singular opinion; semicolons and parentheses where a writer would use them, em-dash sparing. Forbidden: hype adjectives, signposting, hedge stacks, rule-of-three, \\\"In today's world\\\" openers, upbeat conclusions, emoji, bold-for-emphasis. Neutral and patient; explains, does not sell. Conservative mode.\\n\\n**2. Plain / clear (Zinsser)** - tight modern nonfiction. Short words over long, cut every clutter phrase, concrete nouns and active verbs, one thought per sentence. Good for docs, READMEs, business prose. Conservative unless source is first-person.\\n\\n**3. Conversational essay** - first-person, contractions, asides, real rhythm; reacts to facts rather than only reporting them; lets some mess in (tangents, half-formed thoughts). Implies drastic mode. Only for content that already carries a personal voice - never forced onto encyclopedic text.\\n\\n**4. Journalistic / news** - AP style: short lede carrying the key fact first, inverted pyramid, attributed claims (\\\"according to…\\\"), plain verbs, no editorializing. Conservative.\\n\\nIf the user names a profile, rewrite toward it. Otherwise infer it per *Style, chosen silently*.\\n\\n## Matching the user's own voice\\n\\nWhen the user provides a writing sample (inline or a file path), take the target voice from it instead of a profile: measure its sentence lengths and their variation, formality, paragraph openers, punctuation habits, recurring phrases - then rebuild the cleaned text with those habits. No sample: fall back to *Content type and voice* below.\\n\\n## Content type and voice\\n\\nThe biggest failure mode is injecting authorial voice into content that has none by design. A Wikipedia article does not get to say \\\"I keep coming back to...\\\" Classify first:\\n\\n**Encyclopedic / factual** - third-person, dense with proper nouns, dates, statistics, citations, technical vocabulary (Wikipedia, science, news, docs, specs). → **Conservative mode.** Remove AI patterns, vary rhythm, keep tone neutral. Do not add first-person stance, opinions, or asides. Keep formal contractions (`it is`, `do not`) as-is.\\n\\n**Opinion / first-person** - already uses \\\"I\\\" or \\\"we\\\"; expresses stance; has takes and asides (essays, posts, reviews). → **Drastic mode.** Remove AI patterns AND add voice. Contract where natural (`it's`, `don't`, `you've`).\\n\\n**Mixed or unclear** → default to conservative.\\n\\n**Classification signals:** first-person markers above ~2 % of words → opinion. Proper-noun density above ~5 %, numbers / dates / citations present → encyclopedic. Imperative voice → conservative (technical).\\n\\n### Adding voice (drastic mode only)\\n\\nTake positions - respond to facts instead of only stating them. Let rhythm move irregularly: a blunt sentence, then one that unwinds at its own pace; the enemy is a metronome in either direction. Acknowledge complexity, use \\\"I\\\" where it fits, and leave the small irregularities a person would - a digression, a parenthetical, an idea carried only as far as it needs to go. Be specific about feelings rather than generic.\\n\\n## Critical content preservation\\n\\n**Must survive every rewrite:** numbers and units (`0.19`, `1989`, `340 kg/m³`, `55 %`), proper nouns, hyphenated technical terms (`thin-walled`, `load-bearing`, `strength-to-weight`), citations, domain vocabulary.\\n\\n**Safe to paraphrase:** hedges (`typically`, `generally`), generic passive verbs (`results from`, `consists of`), abstract property nouns when the domain term is also present, filler adverbs. *Paraphrasing* a hedge keeps the qualification and changes the wording; deleting the last one turns a qualified claim into an absolute one and is covered below.\\n\\n**Never invent:** dates, statistics, quotes, named individuals, citations to sources not in the input. If specifics are missing, stay vague - do not supply plausible-sounding facts.\\n\\n**Never change what a statement asserts, who it belongs to, or how much of it there is.** These are the failures that survive an entity check, because no number or name moves; every one was found in a real rewrite. Do not:\\n\\n- **Turn a measurement into a claim, or a claim into a measurement.** `The bike weighs 23.2 lb` is the writer's observation. `Claimed weight is 23.2 lb` attributes it to the manufacturer and is a different sentence. Never add *claimed*, *reportedly*, *said to be* or *allegedly* to something stated plainly, and never remove them from something attributed.\\n- **Drop a hedge.** `there is the risk of over-diagnosis` becomes an assertion that over-diagnosis happens if `risk of` is cut. Hedge *stacking* is pattern 54 and gets fixed by removing one hedge, never the last one.\\n- **Invert a criticism.** `its small size makes it more of a deterrent than real theft prevention` is a complaint. Reading it as the reason the lock works reverses the writer's judgment.\\n- **Move a number to a different noun.** `30-hour battery life` is not `30 hours of noise cancellation`. Keep every figure attached to the thing it measured, especially in headings, subject lines and captions, where a reader sees it alone.\\n- **Rewrite a placeholder.** `[Name]`, `{{first_name}}` and `[Your Company]` are merge fields belonging to whatever system will fill them. Leave the token exactly as written, or leave the document alone.\\n- **Add material.** A rewrite fixes what is there. Anything past **1.25x the input word count** is invented, however plausible it sounds: elaboration, a new benefi…230 tokens truncated…d). Never strip legitimate `\\\\t` / `\\\\n` / `\\\\r` inside the body. `scripts/detect.py --clean` applies all four deterministically.\\n\\n## Pattern index\\n\\nEach pattern has a full Before / After in `references/patterns.md` (read it for the precise phrasing list when working a pattern). The numbering groups patterns by function; it folds the community-documented signs together with this project's additions rather than ordering them by origin.\\n\\n### Lexical tells (1-9)\\n1. AI vocabulary (era-variable - refresh per release)\\n2. Model-dialect vocabulary (fires at 2+ combined hits)\\n3. Promotional language\\n4. Hyphenated word-pair overuse (protect technical compounds)\\n5. \\\"Simple yet X\\\" cliché\\n6. Stacked adjective chains (4+ adjectives before one noun)\\n7. Rule of three (generic single-word triads only)\\n8. Synonym cycling\\n9. AI character names in fiction\\n\\n### Rhetorical filler (10-26)\\n10. Significance inflation\\n11. Notability name-dropping\\n12. Vague attributions\\n13. Article-titles-as-proper-nouns\\n14. Conservation / ecosystem padding\\n15. False ranges\\n16. Superficial -ing tail clauses (never flag factual-consequence -ing; list in §16)\\n17. Negative parallelisms and tailing negations\\n18. \\\"Not X, just Y\\\" / \\\"No X, just Y\\\" framing\\n19. Filler phrases\\n20. Empty pivot phrases\\n21. Outcome speculation tails\\n22. Persuasive authority tropes\\n23. Editorial interjections\\n24. Self-thoroughness phrases\\n25. Question-answer rhetorical pattern\\n26. \\\"Concrete evidence\\\" defense phrase\\n\\n### Structure and discourse (27-39)\\n27. Compulsive intro hooks\\n28. Meandering intro - semantic\\n29. Prompt echo (first sentence restates the prompt)\\n30. Diff-anchored writing (describe the thing, not the change) - regex + semantic\\n31. Signposting and announcements\\n32. Cataloguing lead-ins\\n33. Inline-header vertical lists\\n34. Mid-essay bullet injection (bullets where prose belongs)\\n35. Fragmented headers\\n36. Formulaic challenges sections\\n37. Transition cluster overuse (2+ per paragraph)\\n38. Compulsive conclusion phrases\\n39. Generic positive conclusions\\n\\n### Rhythm and cadence (40-46)\\n40. Sentence-length monotony - statistical\\n41. Mechanical sentence-length alternation - statistical\\n42. Opener repetition\\n43. Avoidance of fragments and run-ons (drastic mode only)\\n44. Uniform paragraph length - statistical\\n45. Identical paragraph structure - semantic\\n46. Semicolon and parenthesis underuse (document-level statistical signal)\\n\\n### Register and voice (47-55)\\n47. Chatbot artifacts\\n48. Sycophantic / servile tone\\n49. RLHF / helpful-assistant register - semantic\\n50. Knowledge-cutoff disclaimers\\n51. Copula avoidance\\n52. Passive voice and subjectless fragments\\n53. Two-way passive-voice drift (judge against the genre) - semantic\\n54. Excessive hedging (2+ in one sentence)\\n55. Contraction absence (only in opinion mode)\\n\\n### Formatting and typography (56-61)\\n56. Boldface overuse\\n57. Emojis\\n58. Em-dash overuse (max one per paragraph)\\n59. Title case in headings\\n60. Curly quotation marks\\n61. Hyphen-for-en-dash in numeric ranges\\n\\n### Machine artifacts (62-68), plus a lexical addition (69)\\n62. Invisible and zero-width characters (copy-paste artifacts)\\n63. Placeholder / Mad-Libs text - regex\\n64. Chatbot reference-markup leak - regex\\n65. AI tracking params (utm_source from chat UIs) - regex\\n66. Homoglyph / mixed-script confusables (folded by `--clean`) - character layer\\n67. Non-standard spaces (normalize, do not strip)\\n68. Trailing / stray whitespace (trim from output)\\n69. Canonical marketing-slop phrases - regex, high-precision\\n70. Decorative horizontal rules - 3+ standalone rules in a 25-line document\\n71. Degenerate repetition - over 20% of six-word spans repeated, a model in a loop\\n\\nWhen working on a specific pattern, **read `references/patterns.md` and jump to the matching section** for the exact phrasing list and Before / After.\\n\\n## Deterministic detection\\n\\n**Resolve the detector path first** - `scripts/detect.py` is bundled in the skill directory, not the user's project, so a bare relative path will not resolve. Set `DETECT` once: as a plugin, `$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT/scripts/detect.py`; as a cloned skill, Glob `**/sloptrim/scripts/detect.py` (usually `~/.claude/skills/sloptrim/scripts/detect.py`). Then run it before manual review:\\n\\n```bash\\npython \\\"$DETECT\\\" input.txt        # pass a path (works on all shells)\\n```\\n\\nIt emits JSON: pattern IDs with counts and samples, a `_metrics` block (rhythm statistics, contraction and passive ratios, character-layer counts), and **`ai_tell_score` (0-100)** with a band (`clean` / `light tells` / `mixed` / `heavy tells` / `pervasive tells`). The score weighs pattern diversity over raw density, floors when the character layer finds codepoints that carry no meaning in the text, and ignores copy-editing preferences. It is a triage heuristic, not a calibrated classifier.\\n\\nA score describes the writing in front of it. It is not a judgement about who or what wrote a document, it cannot support one, and it must never be used to accuse a person of anything. See ETHICS.md. The 9 catalogue entries with no detector behind them (7, 9, 28, 29, 43, 45, 49, 52, 53) are worked by reading; even machine-checked ones deserve a reading pass for what the regex misses.\\n\\nFor the deterministic character layer, `--clean` emits the scrubbed text instead of JSON - it strips the invisible and non-printing codepoints, normalizes non-standard spaces to `U+0020`, folds mixed-script homoglyph letters back to ASCII (#66), and trims stray whitespace, leaving visible content untouched:\\n\\n```bash\\npython \\\"$DETECT\\\" --clean gemini_nano_output.txt > clean.txt\\n```\\n\\nRun it on any draft produced by another model to remove smuggled zero-width or TAG-block characters before the text ships.\\n\\n## Positive style patterns\\n\\nRemoving AI patterns is half the job; fill the space with patterns a person writes: active specific verbs (`runs between`, `locks into` - not `serves as`, `provides`); a short pivot sentence every 3-4 sentences that reframes the next idea; em-dash for contrast at most once per paragraph; the colon as an explanation hinge; concrete subjects (\\\"Wind turbine blades rely on balsa\\\" beats \\\"Balsa is used in turbines\\\"); specific judgments (\\\"unusually high stiffness\\\"); a final sentence that carries weight - a fact or real observation, never a generic positive close; varied paragraph length, one-sentence paragraphs allowed.\\n\\n## Reference\\n\\nThe catalogue folds publicly documented signs of AI writing with newer model-specific tells; all worked examples in `references/patterns.md` are original, and the lexical layer (#1) is the refresh point as model vocabularies shift. Scope is rewriting, not detection; defeating institutional integrity systems is out of scope. Version **0.9.0**.\\n/**\\n * Normalize user-facing and legacy reasoning-effort aliases before they cross\\n * a provider protocol boundary. UI labels intentionally remain human-readable,\\n * while Codex and ACP use the compact `xhigh` enum value.\\n */\\nexport function normalizeReasoningEffort(value: string | null | undefined): string | null {\\n  const normalized = value?.trim().toLowerCase() || \\\"\\\";\\n  if (!normalized) {\\n    return null;\\n  }\\n\\n  if (normalized === \\\"extra high\\\" || normalized === \\\"extra-high\\\") {\\n    return \\\"xhigh\\\";\\n  }\\n\\n  return text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/docs/reports/2026-09-12-session-state-audit.md\n+# Session state audit — 12 September 2026\n+\n+The title and effort problems have concrete causes. Several parts of the app can replace the same value, but they do not consistently distinguish a user edit from a default, an old response from a new one, or the requested worker settings from the settings actually running.\n+\n+This report covers 17 findings in session metadata, composer selections, live updates, settings, message delivery, shared subscriptions, and handoffs. It is the requested diagnosis before fixes. No application code was changed. This is a review of the current checkout, including existing uncommitted changes; it is not a claim that every state bug in the repository has been found.\n+\n+Evidence labels below mean:\n+\n+- **Reproduced:** a controlled check executed the relevant existing manager or function and demonstrated the behavior. These were in-memory checks, not changes to real conversations.\n+- **Source-confirmed:** the implementation contains the stated failure path; the complete browser or provider sequence has not been reproduced.\n+- **Conditional risk:** the code permits a failure under the stated conditions, but its occurrence in a real session has not been established.\n+\n+P1 means fix first because the issue can overwrite intent, lose edits, misapply execution settings, or repeat work. P2 means fix next. There are 11 P1 findings and 6 P2 findings.\n+\n+## Findings\n+\n+### 1. P1 — Background title updates can overwrite a manual rename\n+\n+**Source-confirmed; directly relevant to the reported title problem.** The rename endpoint saves only `runs.title`. The background title adopter writes that same column whenever a provider supplies a different acceptable title. There is no saved title owner or manual-rename flag. Its read and write are also separate, so a rename made between them can be overwritten.\n+\n+The database confirms that `title` is the only title-related field. The repair routine also rewrites titles matching its leak heuristics without knowing whether a user deliberately chose them.\n+\n+**Fix:** persist title ownership and a revision. Manual rename must atomically claim ownership. Every automatic writer, including repair and generation, must check that ownership in its SQL update. Emit a named event for manual renames as well as automatic changes. Existing titles cannot all be classified reliably after the fact; preserve ambiguous ones rather than guessing they are automatic.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [rename endpoint](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/routes/runs.ts:385), [automatic overwrite](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/agent-session-title.ts:200), [repair](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/agent-session-title.ts:254), [schema](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/db/schema.ts:21).\n+\n+### 2. P2 — Competing title sources have inconsistent precedence\n+\n+**Source-confirmed, with conditional triggers.** Each worker snapshot can update the parent conversation title. There is no check that this worker owns the conversation title. A usable stream title wins immediately, so a later custom title in the provider's store is not consulted. Different workers or sources can therefore compete.\n+\n+The prompt-echo filter recognizes exact matches and prefixes ending in an ellipsis. A shortened prompt without an ellipsis can pass. This is a possible route back to the beginning of a prompt; I have not identified which provider payload caused your particular flip. The database contains 20 conversations with multiple workers, but that count does not prove title contention occurred in those conversations.\n+\n+**Fix:** define one title-owning worker and explicit source precedence. Preserve the distinction between a provider's custom title and generated title when reading it. Check known fallback formats without rejecting legitimate short summaries indiscriminately.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [source selection](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/snapshots.ts:170), [prompt filter](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/agent-session-title.ts:71), [provider title extraction](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/agent-transcript-title.ts:85).\n+\n+### 3. P1 — Session settings are not stored with each session's composer draft\n+\n+**Reproduced at the manager boundary; browser restoration path source-confirmed.** Text and attachments are saved per session, but model, effort, worker, and account selections are global fields. Switching sessions preserves the message draft but loses unsent setting changes when the selection effect reloads the saved run preferences.\n+\n+The controlled manager check selected Max in A, Low in B, then returned to A; the global effort was still Low. The React effect can subsequently restore A's persisted effort, but cannot restore an unsent choice because it was never saved with A's draft. When a run has no model or effort preference, hydration skips that field, allowing the previous session's value to carry across.\n+\n+**Fix:** store the entire composer selection with the draft under the session ID, with a separate new-conversation draft. Switch the selected session and its composer state in one manager update. Track which fields the user has changed since the last server acknowledgment.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [global selection fields](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeUiStateManager.ts:90), [draft switching](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeUiStateManager.ts:262), [conditional restoration](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useRunSelectionEffects.ts:325).\n+\n+### 4. P1 — Session hydration and effort defaults can overwrite each other\n+\n+**Source-confirmed.** The session restoration effect marks a run hydrated after the first run record it sees. It does not require a fresh snapshot for that run. Later server changes for the same selected run are ignored by the `hydratedRunSelectionId` early return.\n+\n+A separate effect reads effort from local storage whenever the worker/model pair changes. Its special protection for session preferences runs only once per selected session. A later model normalization can therefore replace session effort with a browser default. Local-storage keys identify worker/model pairs, not conversations.\n+\n+**Fix:** make one manager decide when to hydrate, preserve user edits, and accept newer saved preferences. Use browser defaults only when initializing an unset selection. Distinguish automatic catalog refreshes from deliberate model changes.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [one-time hydration](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useRunSelectionEffects.ts:308), [effort restoration and persistence](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeLifecycle.ts:587), [storage key](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/constants.ts:203).\n+\n+### 5. P1 — Model refresh and startup code silently replace chosen models\n+\n+**Startup conversion reproduced; catalog behavior source-confirmed.** `resolveSavedComposerModel(\"claude-opus-5\")` returns `gpt-5.6-sol`. This is an explicit model replacement on startup.\n+\n+Separately, the model normalization effect selects the first available option whenever the selected model is absent. It does not distinguish a complete catalog from fallback options or a refresh still in progress. A temporarily missing model can become a permanent local selection change, which then triggers the effort-default behavior in finding 4.\n+\n+**Fix:** remove the cross-provider saved-model substitution. Keep the selected model while discovery is incomplete. If a complete catalog establishes that the model is unavailable, surface that state and resolve it deliberately rather than silently choosing the first option.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [startup substitution](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/utils.ts:1612), [catalog normalization](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HomeApp.tsx:850), [fallback catalog](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/utils.ts:1626).\n+\n+### 6. P1 — An old HTTP snapshot can overwrite newer live state\n+\n+**Reproduced.** The event cursor rejects old SSE updates, but the polling path ignores whether advancing the cursor succeeded and applies the response anyway. A delayed poll with cursor 10 overwrote a live update with cursor 20 in the controlled check.\n+\n+The state manager also treats server source as sufficient authority to replace run records. Its timestamp comparison is bypassed for server updates. The checksum shortcut protects a local rename only if the entire incoming snapshot is unchanged. Changing an unrelated account in the test made the stale title overwrite the local rename.\n+\n+This can affect titles, status, preferences, and other snapshot fields. The existing generation guard correctly blocks responses after a connection is stopped; it does not solve ordering within an active connection.\n+\n+**Fix:** track snapshot freshness separately from the transport's last received event. Reject or safely merge polls overtaken by newer updates; account for scoped frames and the server's pre-build snapshot anchor. Maintain pending edits per field until an explicit acknowledgment or failure. A checksum is an equality check, not an ordering rule.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [poll response application](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/LiveEventConnectionManager.ts:465), [server merge bypass](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/EventStreamStateManager.ts:201), [checksum shortcut](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/EventStreamStateManager.ts:45).\n+\n+### 7. P1 — Failed mutations restore the whole app to an old snapshot\n+\n+**Source-confirmed.** Move, delete, and archive capture the complete event state and restore it on failure. If live updates or another successful mutation arrive in the meantime, the failed operation overwrites those changes too. This is a rollback of unrelated state, not just the failed action.\n+\n+Rename and move completion also clear shared dialog state without checking whether the user has since opened another dialog. Delete/archive failure restores old rename fields with the same problem.\n+\n+**Fix:** undo only the affected operation's fields or records, and only while that operation still owns the edit. Give dialogs operation IDs so an old completion cannot close or replace a newer editor.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [rename and move](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeMutations.ts:213), [delete rollback](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeMutations.ts:299), [archive rollback](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeMutations.ts:357).\n+\n+### 8. P1 — A settings refresh erases unsaved edits\n+\n+**Reproduced.** Every settings query response calls `SettingsDraftManager.hydrate`, which replaces both baseline and draft and clears all dirty keys. A refresh while editing discards the edits. The settings query does not disable the normal focus-refetch behavior.\n+\n+The controlled check changed a field, hydrated the old server value, and observed the old value with zero dirty fields.\n+\n+**Fix:** refresh the saved baseline while preserving dirty fields. Treat explicit discard and initial hydration as separate operations. Query functions should return data; the manager should decide whether and how that response may update an open draft.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [query side effects](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeQueries.ts:81), [destructive hydration](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/SettingsDraftManager.ts:35).\n+\n+### 9. P1 — Settings save acknowledges values that were never sent\n+\n+**Reproduced using the existing manager calls in the save handler.** Save captures a payload, waits for the network, then reads the current draft and marks that entire draft saved. Changes typed during the request can be marked saved even though the server never received them. The dialog then closes.\n+\n+In the check, the submitted value was `submitted`; the acknowledged baseline became `typed while saving`, with no dirty fields. `markFieldsSaved` also overwrites a newer draft value when acknowledging an older save.\n+\n+**Fix:** return the exact submitted fields and operation revision from the save request. Update the baseline from that acknowledgment, preserve later edits, and leave them dirty. Only close a dialog whose submitted draft still owns the completion.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [save handler](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeMutations.ts:178), [acknowledgment methods](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/SettingsDraftManager.ts:77).\n+\n+### 10. P2 — Overlapping preference saves can undo newer choices\n+\n+**Reproduced.** Planning-review preference saves remember a previous value, then restore it unconditionally on failure. The check requested 2 rounds, then 3 rounds; the newer save succeeded, the older save failed, and the visible value reverted to 1.\n+\n+The shared `isSaving` boolean also becomes false when one request finishes while another remains pending. Workflow settings mutations have similar unconditional rollback behavior.\n+\n+**Fix:** order writes per setting or attach operation revisions to them. Old failures must not undo newer edits. Track pending operations accurately and surface save failure in the UI rather than only in the console.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [planning preferences](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/PlanningReviewPreferencesManager.ts:53), [workflow settings mutations](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/useHomeMutations.ts:189).\n+\n+### 11. P1 — Reported worker effort can disagree with the running provider\n+\n+**Source-confirmed; provider execution not reproduced.** Effort-setting failure during startup is caught and written to stderr, after which startup continues. The requested effort remains recorded. If the provider accepts the setting but omits `configOptions` from its response, the code reads effective effort from the pre-change configuration.\n+\n+Later configuration notifications are appended to output but do not update `record.effectiveEffort`. The explicit configuration API has the same gap. Model/mode status needs the same ownership review because those notifications also carry execution state.\n+\n+**Fix:** separate requested, pending, effective, and rejected settings. Update effective values from provider configuration notifications and acknowledged changes. If the provider cannot confirm a value, keep it unknown and surface the mismatch. Do not report a requested value as proof that it is running.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [startup effort handling](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/manager.ts:1863), [configuration API](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/manager.ts:2089), [notification handling](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/agent-runtime/acp/runtime-client.ts:519).\n+\n+### 12. P2 — Worker-setting comparisons mishandle unknown values and aliases\n+\n+**Source-confirmed, with database evidence.** A model or effort change is recognized only when both the current and requested values are nonempty. An unknown current effort therefore does not trigger reconciliation with an explicit request. The database has 312 workers with a null launch effort whose run has an explicit effort preference; these are historical candidates, not 312 proven failed changes.\n+\n+Comparison also lowercases without normalizing aliases. There are 37 runs and 39 workers storing `extra high`, alongside `xhigh` values. An equivalent choice can look like a change and unnecessarily recreate a worker. Clearing an explicit account to Auto similarly does not count as an account change.\n+\n+**Fix:** use one canonical representation at persistence and comparison boundaries. Distinguish unknown from equal and an omitted setting from an explicit reset. Reconcile against observed provider state where available. Define whether Auto affects this worker immediately or its next allocation, and communicate that consistently.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [comparison](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/send-message.ts:184), [recovery selection](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/workers/launch-selection.ts:33), [normalizer](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/shared/reasoning-effort.ts:6).\n+\n+### 13. P2 — Sending a message can change settings before the send is accepted\n+\n+**Source-confirmed.** `sendConversationMessage` persists worker preferences before checking that a plan review permits the message or that a usable worker exists. A rejected send can therefore still change saved session preferences.\n+\n+The preference helper also treats absent fields inconsistently: absent effort preserves the old value, while absent model/account can clear them when a worker type is supplied. Model/effort updates without a worker type are ignored. These differences matter for API clients and replayed requests.\n+\n+**Fix:** validate send admissibility before committing preference changes. Define a typed patch contract for omitted, explicit, and reset values. Tie accepted message settings to the accepted operation rather than partially committing a request that later fails.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [preference persistence](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/send-message.ts:1189), [send ordering](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/send-message.ts:1541).\n+\n+### 14. P1 — Retrying an accepted message can create a second message\n+\n+**Source-confirmed; an existing test explicitly expects this behavior.** When a supplied client message ID already exists, `resolveUserMessageId` generates a new ID. If the first send persisted but its response was lost, retrying that same request creates another user message instead of recovering the result of the first one. Depending on the delivery path, this can repeat agent work.\n+\n+**Fix:** make message submission idempotent under the run and client message ID. Matching retries should return or resume the existing operation; reuse with different content or ownership should be rejected. Persist delivery progress so partial failures can recover safely.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [ID replacement](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/conversations/send-message.ts:1162), [test expecting replacement](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/server/conversations/client-message-id.test.ts:59).\n+\n+### 15. P2 — Shared selectors can return data for the previous context\n+\n+**Source-confirmed.** The client-side `useManagerSelector` cache keys only on the manager's state object. If a component changes its selector because its project, worker, or entry changed, the hook can return the previous selection while the manager state is unchanged. The server-side version already checks selector identity; the client version does not.\n+\n+Affected callers include project workspace controls and worker-entry content. This can show information for the previous context until another manager update happens. Existing selector tests cover server rendering, not this client transition.\n+\n+**Fix:** include selector identity in the client cache, and ensure changes to the manager or equality function cannot reuse an invalid selection. Add a client rerender test that changes only the selected context.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [cache](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/lib/use-manager-snapshot.ts:32), [project-dependent selector](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/home/BranchWorkspaceButton.tsx:120), [entry-dependent selector](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/WorkerEntryContentUrlManager.ts:129).\n+\n+### 16. P2 — Scoped snapshots cannot remove obsolete child records\n+\n+**Reproduced at the merge boundary.** For a selected run that remains present, partial catalog merging unions old and incoming workers, plans, and sessions. A worker absent from the new selected-run frame survives in the client. The controlled check reproduced that retention.\n+\n+The server sends the selected run's workers but marks only the overall catalog partial. The protocol does not clearly declare completeness for each child collection, so the client cannot safely infer which missing records were removed. A later complete catalog can correct this; that still leaves stale state between updates.\n+\n+**Fix:** declare collection completeness per run or send explicit removals. Replace only the declared complete scope and retain unrelated runs. Do not solve this by treating every absent item as deleted.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [merge](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/EventStreamStateManager.ts:154), [scoped server frame](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/runtime/http/routes/events.ts:655).\n+\n+### 17. P2 — Handoff loading can overwrite edits made after opening the dialog\n+\n+**Conditional risk.** Opening the dialog starts `getActive`. The generation guard protects against another open or close, but changing the target/model/effort/account does not change that generation. If an existing handoff returns after an edit, it replaces those new selections. Controls are not disabled for this initial lookup.\n+\n+**Fix:** capture an edit revision as well as the dialog generation. Apply initial loading results only to untouched fields, or finish loading before enabling edits. Preserve the existing protections around prepare and launch.\n+\n+**Evidence:** [initial lookup and setters](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/interface/home/HandoffManager.ts:48), [control availability](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/components/home/CrossCliHandoffDialog.tsx:29).\n+\n+## State-management changes I recommend\n+\n+The app already has managers. The missing piece is consistent ownership and ordering across them. Replacing the state library would not fix these write paths.\n+\n+1. **One manager owns session selection and drafts.** Include model, effort, account, and worker together with the message draft. Keep server preferences, user edits, and observed worker settings distinct.\n+2. **Version mutable records and operations.** Use revisions for titles and preferences, operation IDs for requests and dialogs, and explicit snapshot scope/freshness for live state. Wall-clock timestamps and checksums cannot substitute for all of these.\n+3. **Keep server state plus a small pending-edit layer.** A pending rename should override only its title field. A failed archive should restore only that operation's affected record. Remove whole-app rollback snapshots.\n+4. **Use the existing worker stream for provider evidence.** Reduce configuration events into effective runtime state without adding another transcript or persistence channel.\n+5. **Centralize normalization and capability checks.** Share effort identifiers and model/account reset semantics across the composer, send API, startup, recovery, and provider status. The UI currently cannot restore `none`, `minimal`, or `ultra`; those should be supported only where the actual provider catalog allows them, rather than silently inherited from another session.\n+\n+## Optimizations worth doing after correctness\n+\n+- **Stop rereading all prompt text to reconsider an unchanged title.** Every title candidate currently queries all user messages and worker prompts before checking whether the stored title already matches. Check ownership first, remember the processed candidate with its source revision, and bound any cache. The transcript-title cache currently has no eviction policy.\n+- **Preserve object identity for unchanged records.** `HomeApp` subscribes to the full event state, so changing one record replaces arrays that many derived views depend on. Normalize records by ID, retain unchanged objects, and subscribe each view to the fields it uses. Fix finding 15 before relying more heavily on selectors.\n+- **Make related manager changes atomic.** Selecting a session should publish one coherent selection, not several independent setters followed by effects that repair each other. Batch `SettingsDraftManager.patchFields`, which currently emits once per field.\n+- **Avoid notifications for true no-ops.** `StateManager.patch` always creates a new object. `EventStreamStateManager.updateLocal` creates a new object before its identity check, making that check ineffective. Compare the proposed change before notifying subscribers.\n+- **Move housekeeping out of snapshot reads where practical.** Snapshot construction currently invokes reconciliation and, in enriched reads, synchronization that can write metadata. Keep recovery observable and reliable, but let reads consume already-produced state where possible. This should be measured before and after; no performance improvement has been benchmarked in this audit.\n+\n+## Verification and limits\n+\n+The seven existing test files for event state, live connections, home UI state, settings drafts, lifecycle hooks, selection effects, and mutation ownership passed: **66 tests**. The additional in-memory audit executed nine checks, covering stale polling, rename overwrite, scoped worker retention, settings refresh, incorrect save acknowledgment, global effort storage, overlapping preference saves, unsupported effort restoration, and the saved-model substitution. Passing existing tests therefore does not establish that these update sequences are safe.\n+\n+The database was opened read-only. At inspection time it contained 490 unarchived runs, 37 runs with `extra high`, and 312 workers with missing launch effort despite an explicit run effort. Counts include historical data and do not measure current bug frequency. No live conversations were created, altered, or removed. The test suite used its isolated temporary roots; the custom checks used only in-memory state.\n+\n+The controlled checks are in [the audit script](/tmp/omniharness-state-audit-2026-09-12.ts). Browser interaction timing and actual provider effort acceptance still need focused reproductions during the fixes. I did not perform an exhaustive lifecycle, security, or whole-repository audit. Existing unrelated workspace changes were left intact.\n+\n+I would address manual title ownership, session selection ownership, stale snapshots, and narrow rollback first. Settings acknowledgments and message idempotency belong in that first correctness pass as well. Each fix should add the failing event sequence as a regression; lifecycle changes should use the existing HTTP/SSE harness, and frontend changes must pass `pnpm build:interface:web`.\n*** End Patch"));
